# MIAM-00111 — Qwen3.8-Flash-Next Practical Hosting Path (V3)

**Date:** 2026-09-24  
**Scope:** MIAM-00111 / VM102 test bed, while VM103 remains the known-good production fallback.

## Executive conclusion

The latest handoff shows that **128 GB system RAM is not the main blocker anymore**. The FP8 PLE CPU-offload path worked. The deployment instead failed because stock vLLM 0.30.0 currently blocks the topology that your six 20 GB RTX 3080s need:

- Qwen4Exp/PLE rejects pipeline parallelism in stock vLLM 0.30.0.
- TP2 fits the architecture but not the model weights.
- TP4 almost fits the weights, but leaves too little VRAM headroom on 20 GB cards.
- The tested AWQ artifact then hit an unfused-expert loader shape bug.

So the next path should **not** be “add more RAM” or “try more launch flags.”

The next path is:

> Use a community Qwen3.8-Flash-Next vLLM fork that already has working pipeline-parallel support, use the checkpoint that fork is known to load, and port its PP topology to your six RTX 3080s.

## 1. Preserve production

Keep VM103 unchanged:

```text
Qwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4
TP2/DP3
70K context
known-good
```

Keep VM102 as the isolated Flash-Next test VM.

Do not modify the production `/opt/vllm-venv`.

## 2. Stop using stock vLLM 0.30.0 for this experiment

The current handoff proves the stock path is structurally blocked for your hardware.

Do not spend another session patching random vLLM 0.30 flags.

Use one known-working community base.

### Preferred base

`ahnguyen17/flashnext-vllm`

Why:

- proven PP3;
- Qwen4Exp/PLE PP fixes;
- vision works;
- tool calling works;
- long context works;
- MTP under PP is documented.

### Secondary base

`todiadiyatmo/rtx-3090-vllm-recipe-qwen-flash-next`

Why:

- Ampere-specific;
- pinned vLLM image;
- FP8 PLE host offload;
- proven TP2×PP2 on 4×3090.

### Third reference

`loktar00/qwen38-flash-next-vllm-3090-recipe`

Why:

- strong Ampere benchmark evidence;
- W4A16;
- FP8 KV options;
- useful reference for throughput/concurrency tuning.

## 3. Target topology for MIAM-00111

First target:

```text
TP2 × PP3
DP1
6 GPUs
PLE CPU-offloaded
MTP off initially
```

Map:

```text
PP0 -> GPU0 + GPU1
PP1 -> GPU2 + GPU3
PP2 -> GPU4 + GPU5
```

This is the topology your hardware needs because each card has only 20 GB.

Use an uneven pipeline layer partition if needed so no rank is too close to the 20 GB ceiling.

Aim to keep each rank around:

```text
<=17–18 GB weights/runtime before KV allocation
```

## 4. Use the recipe-native checkpoint

Do not retry the exact same AWQ artifact unless the selected fork explicitly supports its expert-loader format.

Prefer:

1. the checkpoint used by the selected community recipe;
2. FP8 PLE host offload;
3. fused/expected expert layout;
4. only then consider converting/repacking your existing artifact.

Avoid writing another custom unfused-AWQ loader unless there is no other route.

## 5. Stage-zero feasibility gate

Before a long model load, verify:

1. the fork builds/runs;
2. `pipeline_parallel_size=3` is accepted;
3. PLE works with PP;
4. all six GPUs enumerate;
5. TP2×PP3 process groups form;
6. the checkpoint expert format is recognized;
7. PLE is host-offloaded and not replicated six times.

If any of these fail, stop before a full load.

## 6. Bring-up ladder

Start small.

### Stage A

```text
8K context
C1
MTP off
vision retained
prefix caching off initially
```

Require:

- all PP ranks initialize;
- no loader-shape error;
- no GPU OOM;
- PLE offload works;
- `/v1/models` works;
- short completion works.

### Stage B

```text
32K C1
vision smoke test
```

### Stage C

```text
70K C1
65K–68K real prompt
```

### Stage D

```text
C2
C4
C6
C8
```

Record:

- TTFT;
- per-agent tok/s;
- aggregate tok/s;
- host RAM;
- VRAM/rank;
- KV capacity;
- errors/restarts.

Target:

```text
>=20 tok/s/agent
C4 minimum useful
C6–C8 preferred
```

## 7. If TP2×PP3 is close but still OOMs

Try, in this order:

1. uneven PP layer partition;
2. lower-overhead compatible W4A16/AWQ checkpoint;
3. limited CPU weight offload to recover roughly 1–2 GiB/GPU;
4. lower initial KV allocation while proving the model;
5. only after the model works, increase context/concurrency.

Do not remove vision as the primary fix. The handoff measured the vision component as too small to materially solve the VRAM problem.

## 8. Four-GPU control test

If the six-GPU port is ambiguous, run the selected recipe's exact published four-GPU topology as a diagnostic.

This is not the final target.

It answers:

> Does the recipe/checkpoint itself work on MIAM-00111 Ampere hardware?

If yes, then the remaining problem is specifically the six-GPU PP3 adaptation.

## 9. Upstream wait path

If maintaining a community fork is not desirable, wait for upstream vLLM to provide all of:

- Qwen4Exp PLE with PP>1;
- stable Ampere Flash-Next support;
- compatible AWQ/W4A16 MoE loader;
- stable PLE CPU offload under PP;
- PP/MTP fixes.

As of 2026-09-24, upstream support is still actively evolving and there is no dependable ETA.

## 10. Decision

### Fastest path to local Flash-Next

```text
VM102
community PP-enabled vLLM fork
recipe-native W4A16/FP8-PLE checkpoint
TP2×PP3
DP1
128 GB host RAM
6× RTX 3080 20 GB
8K -> 32K -> 70K -> C2/C4/C6/C8
```

### Lowest-maintenance path

Keep VM103 on Qwen3.8-27B and wait for upstream vLLM to absorb the required PP/PLE/loader fixes.

## Recommendation

A real local Flash-Next deployment is still plausible on this hardware.

The next attempt should be a **port of an already-working PP community implementation**, not another stock-vLLM tuning session.

The key question is now whether the PP3 community patches and a compatible checkpoint can be cleanly adapted to **three TP2 stages on six 20 GB RTX 3080 cards**.


Please execute this plan autonomously. You can ask me questions as you go, but if I don't answer, just continue to work.

Always enumerate\n  `GET /api2/json/nodes` and use the exact returned name; guessing yields\n  `500 hostname lookup failed`.\n- **Guest exec (the replacement for `qm guest exec`):** POST\n  `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec` with body\n  `{\"command\":[\"bash\",\"-lc\",\"echo <b64> | base64 -d | bash\"]}`, then poll\n  GET `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` until `exited`; read\n  `exitcode` / `out-data` / `err-data`. TWO traps: (1) urlencode the body with\n  `doseq=True` — a plain urlencode silently DROPS list values, and the exec fails with\n  HTTP 596 / no pid, which looks like a broken guest agent but is a client bug;\n  (2) dispatch returns 500 when the VM is stopped — check vm status first.\n- Guest agent channel sanity probe: GET `/agent/get-osinfo` (cheap, works even when\n  exec routing misbehaves). VM stopped -> 500 with no data.\n- **PVE API parameter formats (2026-09-13, v0.11 session):** three distinct traps in one\n  endpoint family. (1) Array params are REPEATED plain keys, NOT `name[]=` — body\n  `command=bash&command=-lc&command=<script>`; `command[]=...` and JSON-string bodies both\n  400 with `Parameter verification failed`. (2) `agent/exec` does NOT accept a `timeout`\n  param (schema rejects it: `property is not defined in schema`) — dispatch returns a pid\n  and you enforce your own deadline while polling. (3) `/agent/exec` returns\n  `{\"data\":{\"pid\": N}}` (a DICT) — extracting `data['pid']` blindly throws; handle both\n  dict and bare-int shapes. (4) GET endpoints take params in the QUERYSTRING — passing\n  them as a POST-style body yields silent empty responses / 400s.\n- **LXC containers have NO `/lxc/<id>/exec` API endpoint on PVE 9.2** (POST returns\n  `501 Method 'POST /nodes/.../lxc/.../exec' not implemented`) — the qemu guest-agent exec\n  path is VM-only. For CT internals use SSH/node-shell (`pct exec`), or reach the CT's\n  services over the network from a VM that has the tooling (e.g. install\n  `postgresql-client` on the app VM and dump remotely instead of exec'ing into the DB CT).\n- **Root@pam ticket via `/access/ticket` works from a Python urllib client** when the\n  bash+curl path is unavailable (no jq / curl quirks) — same urlencoded form\n  (`username=root@pam`), same cookie/CSRF handling, self-signed TLS needs an\n  `ssl.CERT_NONE` context. Reusable pattern: stage creds in a 0600 two-line file, refresh\n  the ticket on 401 once, then proceed.\n- **`vmstatus` reads `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/status/current`; `vmconfig` reads\n  `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/config`. Both safe read-only.**\n- If cluster-forwarded API calls fail oddly (e.g. HTTP 596), retry against the node's\n  own endpoint `https://10.0.20.<node-digits>:8006` with the same bot ticket.\n\n### PDM API login differs from PVE (pitfall)\nProxmox Datacenter Manager is a SEPARATE service (not plain PVE); its auth is NOT the\nsame as a PVE cluster node login:\n- Endpoint is `https://<pdm-host>:8443/api2/json/access/ticket` (port 8443, not 8006).\n- Username form is `user@LLDAP-Domain` (same realm discovery trick).\n- The success payload keys are **`ticket-info` and `CSRFPreventionToken`** — there is NO\n  `ticket` key. Do not read `data['ticket']`. The `data` value may arrive as a\n  JSON-encoded STRING on some versions — parse defensively (`if isinstance(d, str):\n  d = json.loads(d)`), or a dict access throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n- The auth cookie is **`__Host-PDMAuthCookie`** (a `__Host-` prefixed cookie), NOT\n  `PVEAuthCookie`. Persist it in a cookie jar (`curl -c/-b`) rather than hand-setting.\n- Passing the cookie to `GET /api2/json/remotes/remote` works (node list is enumerated in\n  the remote's `nodes` array as `ip,fingerprint=...`). Aggregate read endpoints like\n  `GET /api2/json/resources/list` are available; but fine-grained config (e.g. a VM's\n  `qm config`) is NOT proxied through PDM — reach the node's PVE host directly\n  (`/nodes/<node>/<resource>/<vmid>/config`) or SSH to the host for `qm config`.\n  - `resources/list` shape: `{\"data\":[{\"remote\":\"<remote-id>\",\"resources\":[...]}]}` — one\n    element per remote; entries are `pve-node` / `pve-storage` / `pve-qemu` / `pve-lxc`\n    with `vmid,node,status,name,tags`. Ideal for cluster-wide VMID/IP/name audits.\n  - Login also works as `jordatech@LLDAP-Domain`; use a curl `-c`/`-b` cookie jar (the\n    `__Host-PDMAuthCookie`) instead of hand-built Authorization headers.\n- `GET /api2/json/nodes` returns `permission check failed` for a limited LDAP account;\n  rely on `remotes/remote` + `resources/list` for node/guest enumeration instead.\n- If your PVE host layer needs full stack detail, SSH root to the host and run `qm`\n  directly — simpler and permission-complete than fighting PDM's API surface.\n\n### PVE API as PRIMARY access path (user directive 2026-09-11: \"Switch primary to the PVE API/web path\")\n\nUse the PVE REST API through an LLDAP bot account as the FIRST choice for cluster\noperations; password-SSH runners (noderun.py) drop to fallback for host-only tasks\nthe API can't reach.\n\n- **Bot creds**: staged as a 0600 file (`~/.pve_ldap_bot`: line 1 = username, line 2 =\n  password; realm suffix appended in code). Never echo, never commit.\n- **Auth**: `POST /api2/json/access/ticket` with urlencoded\n  `username=<user>@LLDAP-Domain&password=...` → then send cookie `PVEAuthCookie` on every\n  call + header `CSRFPreventionToken` for non-GET. TLS is self-signed → ssl context with\n  `check_hostname=False, verify_mode=CERT_NONE`.\n- **Ready-made helper**: `scripts/pve_api.py` (login / realms / nodes / resources /\n  permissions / vmconfig <node> <vmid> / vmstatus <node> <vmid> / raw <METHOD> <path> [body];\n  also importable: `from pve_api import api, load_creds`).\n- **Node short-names are INCONSISTENT** — ring nodes are `miam00111/miam00112/miam00143/\n  miam00144` (no hyphen) while `miam-00135`, `miam-00100`, `miam-00149` carry hyphens.\n  NEVER guess a node name in an API path; enumerate `GET /api2/json/nodes` first.\n- **Guest exec via API** (replaces qm guest exec + most SSH-to-VM work):\n  `POST /nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec` with body\n  `{\"command\": [\"bash\", \"-lc\", \"echo <b64-of-script> | base64 -d | bash\"]}`, then poll\n  `GET .../agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` until `exited` (fields: exitcode, out-data,\n  err-data). **PITFALL: urlencode the body with `doseq=True`** — the `command` value is a\n  LIST, and plain urlencode silently DROPS list values, so the POST returns 200-ish while\n  the exec never runs (dispatch returns no pid / 596-looking failures).\n- **VM lifecycle** via `/nodes/<n>/qemu/<id>/status/start` (and stop/reboot) is clean and\n  audited by the bot identity; track completion via `/nodes/<n>/tasks/<upid>/status`.\n- **PDU power portal** (Jordan, 2026-09-11): web-managed PDU portal at\n  `https://10.0.20.154/` (nginx front; gunicorn binds 127.0.0.1:5000 — :5000 from outside\n  is refused; `http://` 301s to https). Creds staged in `~/.pdu_portal` (600).\n  **Login quirks:** the LLDAP username/password form REJECTED the staged root creds\n  (2026-09-11); the **emergency local administrator** form works — POST `/login` with\n  `mode=emergency`, `e_user`, `e_pass`. The post-login redirect Location is RELATIVE\n  (`/`) — resolve against the base URL or requests/urllib throws MissingSchema. The\n  first dashboard GET can exceed 10s; use a 30s timeout. Outlet rows carry `data-ip`,\n  `data-outlet`, and a label div (153/7 = MIAM-00147 NUC, 153/8 = MIAM-00148 ThunderBay)\n  — verify live labels before cycling. Control-plane only — power telemetry polling\n  stays on Emporia/GPU NVML per the standing rule (never poll PDU network endpoints\n  .151-.153 for telemetry).\n\n### PVE host/guest shell without sshpass (pexpect) + cloud-init & serial recipes\n\nWhen you must drive Proxmox hosts/VMs from a LAN Hermes box (no web GUI, no existing\ninjected key for the host, possibly no DHCP on the segment, optionally offline hosts),\nthe reusable recipes live in `references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md`, and a ready-made\ndrain-safe password-SSH script runner is `scripts/ssh_run.py` (runs a local .sh on a\nnode, saves output; reads the credential from the profile memory file at runtime and\nnever prints it). Key points:\n- pexpect (user-level `~/ssh-venv`) replaces missing `sshpass`; key-based variant for VMs.\n  Ready-made drain-loop runner: `scripts/ssh_run.py` (see scripts dir; handles the\n  empty/truncated `child.before` pitfall and reads the password from profile memory).\n  **Password auth REQUIRES pexpect** — `subprocess.run(..., input=PW)` fails silently\n  (Permission denied) because the password prompt appears AFTER stdin is consumed.\n  If `sshpass` is absent and uninstalled, port the loop to pexpect, never to input-feed.\n- ssh_run.py takes **IP addresses or full resolvable names, not bare node short-names**\n  (`miam00112` → \"Could not resolve hostname\"; use `10.0.20.112`).\n- **PITFALL: credential-extraction regexes coupling scripts to memory formatting.**\n  ssh_run-style scripts that parse the profile MEMORY.md for the password (`r\"root/(\\S+?)\\)\"`\n  etc.) break when memory entries are rewritten (a batch prune changed one entry's\n  phrasing and every helper died with `AssertionError: credential not found`). Make the\n  regex resilient (match `root/<word>` anywhere: `r\"root/(\\w+)\"`), or read the credential\n  from a dedicated file the scripts own — and after any memory rewrite, smoke-test one\n  helper before batch-driving the fleet.\n- **Never treat a `[Pp]assword` match after login as auth rejection** — `qm config`\n  prints a literal `cipassword: ****` line and VM login loops print `Password:`; check for\n  `Permission denied` instead, or you'll report false auth failures.\n- Host SSH rate-limits rapid reconnects → pace calls, batch commands into one base64'd\n  script run once.\n- **PITFALL: pexpect `child.before` empty/truncated on long batched output.** A single\n  `expect(EOF)` + one `child.before` read can return EMPTY (match at buffer start) or\n  lose most output. Drain instead: loop\n  `expect([r'[Pp]assword:', pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT])` until EOF, appending\n  `child.before` on every iteration, then `\"\".join()` the pieces. Keep the password\n  pattern inside the loop so late prompts still match; treat success as the absence of\n  `Permission denied` in the joined text.\n- Large binaries to offline hosts: serve via `python3 -m http.server` on the internet-\n  capable LAN host, `curl` inside the Proxmox host. pexpect-scp with `encoding='utf-8'`\n  corrupts binaries.\n\n### `qm guest exec` / `pct exec` / `pct push` only work on the OWNING node\n\n`/etc/pve` is cluster-readable, but guest-exec commands resolve the guest config against\nthe LOCAL node. Running `qm guest exec 103` from a node that doesn't own VM103 fails with\n`Configuration file 'nodes/<wrong-node>/qemu-server/103.conf' does not exist`. SSH to the\nowning node first (fleet map: VM103→miam-00111, VM401→miam-00112, VM109→miam-00143,\nVM111→miam-00144; CT115→miam-00147). `pct push` additionally requires the CT to be running.\n\n**PITFALL: `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -s` does NOT forward stdin.** A heredoc piped at\n`qm guest exec` feeds the node-side qm process, not the guest — the call returns\n`{\"exitcode\":0}` with EMPTY `out-data` while the guest script never ran. This silently\nno-ops entire batch installs. The reliable pattern is base64-in-command:\n`qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -c \"echo <base64> | base64 -d | bash\"`.\nSame class of confusion: `scp node:/tmp/x` lands the file on the NODE — to move a file\nnode→CT use `pct push <ctid> /tmp/x /tmp/x` (and `pct pull` to fetch out).\n\n### Inspecting the GUEST vs the NODE (verify your vantage point)\n\nA script run directly through SSH lands on the NODE shell — its output (`pve-root` df,\nQEMU process list, host `lspci`, missing `nvidia-smi`) describes the hypervisor, NOT the\nguest. This burned a full diagnostic round: a \"bare guest\" verdict was drawn from node-shell\noutput while the guest was actually fully built (drivers + venv + model cache intact).\nRule: every guest-internal probe (cloud-init status, nvidia-smi, venv/model dirs, guest FS\nusage, guest internet) MUST be wrapped in `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -lc '...'` on the\nowning node. Before mutating anything based on an inspection, state which vantage point\nproduced the evidence.\n\n### Deploying a file INTO a VM guest when your box has no direct key\n\n1. base64 the deploy script locally; run it to the owning node over SSH, decode to /tmp there.\n2. From the node: `scp /tmp/x.sh <guest-user>@<guest-ip>:/tmp/` — the node holds the SSH\n   keypair baked into the guest's cloud-init `authorized_keys` (e.g. the `root@miam-00135`\n   RSA key referenced in cloud-config users).\n3. From the node: `ssh <guest-user>@<guest-ip> \"sudo bash /tmp/deploy.sh\"` — the cloud-init\n   user has NOPASSWD sudo. Direct SSH from your own box only works if YOUR key is in the\n   guest's authorized_keys; otherwise this node-relay path is the reliable route.\n\n### Debian 12 LXCs ship no sudo\n\n`sudo: command not found` inside a fresh Debian 12 CT. As root use\n`runuser -u <user> -- <cmd>` (e.g. `runuser -u postgres -- psql ...`). Locale/UTF-8 perl\nwarnings in these CTs are harmless noise.\n\n### Reconstructing an undocumented service into systemd from bash_history\n\nWhen a guest runs a critical service (e.g. vLLM) with no unit file, recover the final\nbattle-tested invocation from `grep -ihE '<service>' /root/.bash_history /home/*/.bash_history`\n—the LAST occurrence is the current working config. Turn it into\n`/etc/systemd/system/<svc>.service` (Environment lines for exports like HF_HOME, ExecStart\nwith all flags, Restart=on-failure, TimeoutStartSec generous for model load), then\n`daemon-reload && enable --now`, and verify via the service's HTTP endpoint\n(`/v1/models` + a small chat completion for vLLM) before calling it up.\n- Read a headless guest's console via `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0`\n  under `pty.fork()` — the only view before the guest agent/network are up. Verified\n  working pattern: `pty.fork()` + `os.execvp('socat', ...)`, write `\\n`, then\n  `select()`-loop reads for ~20s; a booting/idle guest yields the `login:` banner.\n  Wrap it in a script landed on the node via base64-heredoc, then `python3 <script>`\n  — nodes have no pexpect installed (stdlib `pty`/`select` only).\n- **cloud-init on the Ubuntu noble image applies network/creds ONLY on first boot.**\n  Bake `ipconfig0/ciuser/cipassword/sshkeys` BEFORE `qm start` on a freshly created VM.\n  Patching a booted VM then `qm reboot` does NOT re-apply them.\n  ### Re-run WITHOUT recreating: bump the cloud-init instance-id\n  When a first boot half-applied (ssh/packages missing, wrong IP) you do NOT need to\n  destroy/rebuild: edit the user snippet (`/var/lib/vz/snippets/<name>.yaml`), bump\n  `instance-id` in the meta snippet (e.g. `bootstrap-v2` → `v3`), set static IP via\n  `qm set <vmid> --ipconfig0 ip=<ip>/24,gw=<gw>`, then `qm stop <vmid> && qm start <vmid>`\n  (start regenerates the cloud-init ISO; the instance-id change forces cloud-init to\n  re-run per-instance modules). Poll readiness with `timeout 2 bash -c 'echo > /dev/tcp/<ip>/22'`\n  + `qm agent <vmid> ping`, then verify `cloud-init status --long` = done/no errors.\n  ### Static vs DHCP on vmbr0: VERIFY before asserting\n  Do NOT assume the LAN lacks DHCP — verified 2026-09 this vmbr0 /24 HAS a DHCP\n  server (a cloud-init `ip=dhcp` VM leased an address; all Hermes LXCs run `ip=dhcp`).\n  **After any build assigns statics, run the full audit in\n  `references/network-ip-collision-audit.md`** (asset-sheet MAC cross-check + ARP\n  classification + OPNsense DHCP pool boundary check via config.xml download) — the\n  user explicitly asks for this double-check, and it caught the corosync collision\n  below plus stale .115 references left in app configs.\n  Still assign STATIC IPs to infrastructure roles (managers/gateways/DB) so identities\n  never drift, but prove an address is free before assigning:\n  1) `qm list`/`pct list` per node + `pvesh get /cluster/nextid`\n  2) `grep -rE '10\\.0\\.20\\.[0-9]+' /etc/pve/qemu-server/ /etc/pve/lxc/`\n  3) `ip neigh show` + parallel ping sweep of the /24\n  4) **CLUSTER-WIDE: also check `/etc/pve/corosync.conf` `ring0_addr` values** — bare-metal\n  cluster nodes own

GET params must go in the querystring for PVE\n37|            path = path + ('&' if '?' in path else '?') + enc.decode()\n38|        else:\n39|            data = enc\n40|    if method != 'GET':\n41|        headers['CSRFPreventionToken'] = csrf\n42|    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + path, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)\n43|    try:\n44|        resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=180)\n45|        return json.load(resp)\n46|    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:\n47|        if e.code == 401 and retry:\n48|            _login()\n49|            return api(method, path, body, retry=False)\n50|        raise\n51|\n52|\n53|def find_vm(node_hint=None, vmid=None, ip=None):\n54|    \"\"\"Locate a VM/LXC by vmid or current IP (via guest agent if possible).\"\"\"\n55|    vms = []\n56|    nodes = [n['node'] for n in api('GET', '/api2/json/nodes')['data']]\n57|    for node in nodes:\n58|        for vm in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu')['data']:\n59|            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': vm['vmid'], 'name': vm.get('name', ''), 'status': vm['status'], 'type': 'qemu'})\n60|        for ct in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc')['data']:\n61|            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': ct['vmid'], 'name': ct.get('name', ''), 'status': ct['status'], 'type': 'lxc'})\n62|    return vms\n63|\n64|\n65|def guest_exec(node, vmid, command, timeout=120):\n66|    \"\"\"Run command in guest via qemu-guest-agent, wait for completion, return (stdout, stderr, exitcode).\n67|    PVE expects 'command' as repeated form fields (arg encode_array).\"\"\"\n68|    # PVE array convention: repeat the parameter name with each value\n69|    # NOTE: 'timeout' is NOT an accepted param for agent/exec (400s); the exec returns a pid\n70|    parts = []\n71|    for c in command:\n72|        parts.append('command=' + urllib.parse.quote(c, safe=''))\n73|    body = '&'.join(parts).encode()\n74|    url = PVE + f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec'\n75|    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n76|    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}', 'CSRFPreventionToken': csrf,\n77|               'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded'}\n78|    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=body, headers=headers, method='POST')\n79|    resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=timeout + 30)\n80|    pdata = json.load(resp)['data']\n81|    pid = pdata['pid'] if isinstance(pdata, dict) else pdata\n82|    import time\n83|    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n84|    while time.time() < deadline:\n85|        time.sleep(2)\n86|        try:\n87|            st = api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec-status', {'pid': pid})['data']\n88|        except Exception:\n89|            continue\n90|        if st.get('exited'):\n91|            return st.get('out-data', ''), st.get('err-data', ''), st.get('exitcode', -1)\n92|    return '', 'TIMEOUT', -1\n93|\n94|\n95|def lxc_exec(node, vmid, command):\n96|    r = api('POST', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc/{vmid}/exec', {'command': json.dumps(command)})\n97|    # LXC exec via API returns after completion\n98|    return r\n99|\n100|\n101|if __name__ == '__main__':\n102|    cmd = sys.argv[1]\n103|    if cmd == 'find':\n104|        for v in find_vm():\n105|            print(f\"{v['vmid']:>5} {v['type']:<4} {v['status']:<7} {v['node']:<12} {v['name']}\")\n106|    elif cmd == 'exec':\n107|        node, vmid, command = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4]\n108|        out, err, code = guest_exec(node, vmid, ['bash', '-lc', command])\n109|        print(out)\n110|        if err: print('STDERR:', err, file=sys.stderr)\n111|        sys.exit(0 if code == 0 else code)\n112|    elif cmd == 'api':\n113|        method, path = sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]\n114|        body = json.loads(sys.argv[4]) if len(sys.argv) > 4 else None\n115|        print(json.dumps(api(method, path, body), indent=2)[:8000])\n116|", "total_lines": 115, "file_size": 4894, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

I also see a zero-downtime staging opportunity: VM102 can boot **without its GPUs** (stripped config) at capped RAM while VM103 keeps serving — doing the slow parts (checkpoint download, fork build, docker pull) with production untouched, then a single clean handoff to GPUs for the actual test.

Preferred isolation: create a separate Flash-Next test VM\n\nA separate VM is preferred because the Flash-Next stack requires unusual pinned vLLM patches, container settings, host-RAM PLE offload, and possibly different NVIDIA/CUDA requirements.\n\nCreate a new VM by cloning the **OS/runtime baseline** of VM103.\n\nDo not change:\n\n```text\nVM103 VMID\nVM103 guest hostname\nVM103 IP\nVM103 DNS/certificates\nVM103 production runtime\n```\n\nThe new test VM receives:\n\n```text\nnew VMID\nnew display name\nnew MAC\ntemporary IP\nonboot = 0 initially\n```\n\nSuggested display label:\n\n```text\nMIAM-00111-QWEN38-FLASHNEXT-TEST\n```\n\n## Important passthrough constraint\n\nThe six GPUs can be assigned to only one active VM at a time.\n\nTherefore:\n\n```text\nVM103 OFF\nFlash-Next test VM ON\n```\n\nor:\n\n```text\nFlash-Next test VM OFF\nVM103 ON\n```\n\nNever start both with the same passthrough devices.\n\nThe test VM is a clean reversible switch, not a concurrently running sibling.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Clone only what is necessary\n\nPreferred approach:\n\n1. clone the VM103 system/root disk;\n2. leave the production VM103 disk untouched;\n3. attach or move the existing 250 GB Flash-Next model/cache disk to the test VM while VM103 is off;\n4. give the test VM the six GPU passthrough devices;\n5. give it the Mellanox/other devices only if actually required;\n6. keep the test VM's network identity separate.\n\nDo **not** duplicate hundreds of gigabytes of model data unnecessarily if the existing staged model disk can be attached exclusively to the test VM.\n\nDocument every disk move so rollback is one command sequence.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Guest RAM budget with 128 GB physical RAM\n\nThe host must retain enough RAM for Proxmox and other host services.\n\n## Initial target\n\nPrefer:\n\n```text\nFlash-Next test VM: 100–104 GB RAM\nballooning: off\n```\n\nOnly increase toward:\n\n```text\n108 GB\n```\n\nif host `MemAvailable`, other guests, and QEMU overhead make it safe.\n\nDo not assign all physical RAM to the guest.\n\n## Host target\n\nTry to preserve roughly:\n\n```text\n20–24 GB class headroom\n```\n\nfor:\n\n```text\nProxmox host\nQEMU overhead\nfilesystem/page cache\nnetwork/storage services\nsafety margin\n```\n\nIf other VMs are running on MIAM-00111, inventory them first.\n\nOnly stop or migrate **nonessential** guests.\n\nDo not stop an unidentified workload merely to gain RAM.\n\n---\n\n# 6. That is not the same as removing vision.\n\nAfter text serving is stable, run a real image prompt before production promotion.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Runtime: vLLM, but isolated and pinned\n\nUse vLLM.\n\nDo **not** try to merge this into `/opt/vllm-venv`.\n\nPreferred runtime:\n\n```text\nDocker / NVIDIA Container Toolkit\npinned vLLM nightly\nexact Ampere recipe patchset\nexact image digest\n```\n\nPrimary reference:\n\n```text\nhttps://github.com/todiadiyatmo/rtx-3090-vllm-recipe-qwen-flash-next\n```\n\nThe reference recipe pins:\n\n```text\nvLLM commit/nightly identity\nAmpere FP8 KV support\nFP8 PLE lookup\npipeline-parallel fixes\nMTP-under-PP fixes\nQwen4Exp support\n```\n\nRecord:\n\n```text\ngit commit\nDocker image digest\npatch SHA256SUMS\ncheckpoint revision\nchat-template revision\nNVIDIA driver\nCUDA version\n```\n\nDo not use `pip install -U vllm` as a deployment method for this experiment.\n\n---\n\n# 9. Critical PLE gate\n\nBefore benchmarking anything, confirm from logs that the runtime resolves the Engram/PLE table as CPU-offloaded.\n\nExpected behavior is equivalent to:\n\n```text\nPLE / Engram CPU offload enabled\n```\n\nand host memory should resemble **one approximately 48 GiB-class FP8 PLE allocation**, not six 17.5 GB worker copies.\n\nMonitor:\n\n```bash\nfree -h\nps -eo pid,cmd,rss --sort=-rss | head -30\ncat /proc/meminfo\ndmesg -T | grep -Ei 'oom|out of memory|killed process'\n```\n\n## Hard stop condition\n\nIf total PLE-related RSS again grows toward:\n\n```text\n~100+ GB\n```\n\nbefore normal model/runtime overhead:\n\n```text\nSTOP\n```\n\nDo not solve a duplicated-PLE software problem by squeezing the host harder.\n\n---\n\n# 10. Use all six GPUs — topology test strategy\n\nThe old failure used:\n\n```text\nTP2 / DP3 / EP\n```\n\nThat should **not** be the first retry because DP created multiple worker/process copies and exposed the PLE duplication problem.\n\nThe Ampere reference is validated on:\n\n```text\nTP2 × PP2\n```\n\nacross four 3090s.\n\nFor six 3080s, test topology conservatively.\n\n## Candidate A — TP2 × PP3 + EP\n\nPreferred first hypothesis:\n\n```text\nTP = 2\nPP = 3\nDP = 1\nEP = enabled\nMTP = off\nall 6 GPUs\n```\n\nWhy:\n\n- model has 2 KV heads, making TP2 natural;\n- PP distributes layers over all six GPUs;\n- DP1 avoids three model/PLE copies;\n- 20 GB cards have less per-GPU VRAM than the reference 24 GB 3090s, so PP3 may help per-stage fit.\n\nThis is **not yet a validated published topology**.\n\nBefore a long startup, verify that the pinned patchset accepts `pipeline_parallel_size=3`.\n\nIf PP3 requires extensive new source changes, stop and move to Candidate B rather than creating a large custom fork.\n\n## Candidate B — TP1 × PP6 + EP\n\nIf supported cleanly:\n\n```text\nTP = 1\nPP = 6\nDP = 1\nEP = enabled\n```\n\nThis distributes layers even more finely.\n\nTrade-offs:\n\n```text\nmore pipeline bubbles\nless tensor communication\npotentially easier VRAM fit\nmore pipeline-stage complexity\n```\n\nTest only if the selected vLLM patchset supports the stage count without invasive changes.\n\n## Candidate C — TP2 × PP2 on four GPUs\n\nUse the published topology as a **recipe-fidelity control**.\n\n```text\nTP = 2\nPP = 2\n4 GPUs active\n2 GPUs idle\n```\n\nBecause RTX 3080 cards have 20 GB rather than 24 GB, this may fail from per-GPU VRAM pressure.\n\nIts purpose is diagnostic:\n\n> Does the published Ampere recipe work on this host at all?\n\nIf Candidate C works but six-GPU variants fail, the problem is topology adaptation, not the basic model/runtime.\n\n---\n\n# 11. Do not use data parallelism initially\n\nDo not begin with:\n\n```text\nDP3\n```\n\nor any topology that creates multiple complete model/PLE worker groups.\n\nFirst get **one model instance** stable across the six GPUs.\n\nConcurrency should initially come from:\n\n```text\nvLLM continuous batching\nmax_num_seqs\nKV/recurrent-state capacity\n```\n\nnot multiple DP copies.\n\nOnce shared PLE behavior is proven, DP can be revisited in a later experiment if there is a strong reason.\n\n---\n\n# 12. GPU-to-GPU communication\n\nThe Ampere reference recipe found GPU P2P materially improved prefill performance but changed decode less.\n\nBefore serving:\n\n```bash\nnvidia-smi topo -m\n```\n\nIf available, run the CUDA P2P bandwidth/latency sample.\n\nRecord whether GPU P2P is actually working.\n\nIf P2P is not available:\n\n```text\ndo not pretend it is\nconfigure NCCL accordingly\n```\n\nA correct PCIe fallback is better than a broken P2P configuration.\n\nDo not patch the production VM's NVIDIA driver for P2P as part of the first Flash-Next bring-up.\n\nProve the model first.\n\n---\n\n# 13. Bring-up ladder\n\nDo not begin with 70K and high concurrency.\n\n## Stage 1 — 8K C1\n\nStart with:\n\n```text\nmax-model-len = 8192\nmax-num-seqs = 1\nMTP = off\nvision model preserved\n```\n\nRequire:\n\n```text\ncontainer healthy\n/v1/models succeeds\nshort text response coherent\nno OOM\nPLE offload correct\nall intended GPUs active\n```\n\n## Stage 2 — vision smoke\n\nWith the same model instance:\n\n```text\none image\nshort question\nsmall output\n```\n\nVerify:\n\n```text\nprocessor works\nvision encoder works\nno unexpected memory explosion\n```\n\nThe reference recipe supports bounded vision requests.\n\n## Stage 3 — 32K C1\n\nRaise:\n\n```text\nmax-model-len = 32768\n```\n\nRun a real ~30K prompt.\n\n## Stage 4 — 70K C1\n\nRaise:\n\n```text\nmax-model-len = 70000\n```\n\nRun:\n\n```text\n65K–68K actual tokenizer tokens\n64–128 output\n```\n\nOnly after this passes should concurrency tuning begin.\n\n---\n\n# 14. MTP strategy\n\nKeep MTP **off** initially.\n\nThe Ampere reference shows:\n\n```text\nMTP off:\nmuch larger logical KV pool\nlower single-stream decode\n\nMTP n=2:\nhigher single-stream decode\nroughly half the KV pool\n```\n\nThe local goal is many concurrent Hermes agents, so KV capacity is more valuable initially.\n\nAfter stable concurrency is known, one optional comparison may test:\n\n```text\nMTP n=2\n```\n\nDo not make it the initial production default.\n\n---\n\n# 15. Concurrency tuning\n\nWith 70K context working, test:\n\n```text\nC1\nC2\nC4\nC6\nC8\n```\n\nFor each run record:\n\n```text\nTTFT p50/p95\nper-agent output tok/s\naggregate output tok/s\nRAM\nVRAM per GPU\nKV capacity\nrequest failures\nGPU utilization\nCPU utilization\n```\n\nPrimary target:\n\n```text\n>=20 output tok/s per active agent\n```\n\nPreferred outcome:\n\n```text\nC6–C8 stable\n```\n\nDo not expect system RAM alone to determine this.\n\nOnce the model fits, concurrency is increasingly controlled by:\n\n```text\nGPU KV/recurrent-state memory\nGPU compute\nbatch scheduler\npipeline efficiency\n```\n\n---\n\n# 16. What 128 GB RAM can realistically accomplish\n\nIf the corrected runtime behaves similarly to the published FP8-PLE Ampere recipe:\n\n```text\n~85 GiB total process/RAM use\n```\n\nthen a 100–108 GB guest is plausible, though tight.\n\nThis leaves approximately:\n\n```text\n15–20+ GB class guest headroom\n```\n\ndepending on exact topology, vision workload, process count, and page cache.\n\nThe host itself must still retain enough memory for Proxmox.\n\nTherefore:\n\n> **128 GB physical RAM is a reasonable software-first target, but this must be proven by measurement on the six-3080 topology.**\n\nNo RAM purchase is part of V2.\n\n---\n\n# 17. If RAM remains the bottleneck\n\nIf the PLE is confirmed non-duplicated but the VM still OOMs:\n\nfirst determine where the memory went.\n\nCapture:\n\n```text\nper-process RSS\nshared memory\npage cache\nPLE worker RSS\nQEMU overhead\nguest kernel memory\nmodel-loader peak\n```\n\nThen try, in order:\n\n1. remove unnecessary test processes;\n2. stop/migrate nonessential guests on MIAM-00111;\n3. reduce build/compile caches during serving;\n4. use the FP8 PLE checkpoint, not BF16 PLE;\n5. reduce initial context while debugging;\n6. reduce `max_num_seqs`;\n7. consider mmap-backed PLE only if supported by the selected vLLM build and proven stable.\n\nDo not purchase RAM as an automatic response.\n\n---\n\n# 18. Multimodal production target\n\nThe final target must preserve:\n\n```text\ntext\nvision/images\nreasoning\ntool calling\nlong context\n```\n\nSuggested bounded vision setting should follow the selected validated recipe.\n\nThe current 3090 recipe has been validated with vision enabled and a bounded image count.\n\nRecord the final:\n\n```text\nimage limit\nmax_pixels\nprocessor revision\n```\n\nin LLM Manager history.\n\n---\n\n# 19. LLM Manager deployment identity\n\nCreate a distinct model/deployment:\n\n```text\nqwen3.8-flash-next\n```\n\nDo not overwrite:\n\n```text\nqwen3.8-27b\ncode\n```\n\nuntil Flash-Next passes final acceptance.\n\nDuring testing, route only explicit test requests to Flash-Next.\n\nAfter promotion, alias changes are a separate controlled action.\n\n---\n\n# 20. LLM Manager history fields for Flash-Next\n\nStore with every successful or failed attempt:\n\n```text\nVMID\nguest RAM\nphysical host RAM\nGPU count/type/VRAM\nartifact/revision/checksum\nvision enabled\nPLE format\nPLE CPU-offload mode\nPLE RSS\nvLLM commit\nDocker digest\npatchset checksum\nTP\nPP\nDP\nEP\nMTP\ncontext\nmax_num_seqs\nKV dtype\nGPU-memory-utilization\nP2P state\nTTFT\nper-agent tok/s\naggregate tok/s\nRAM peak\nVRAM peak per GPU\nHermes tool result\nvision smoke result\nstatus\nfailure reason\n```\n\nFailed runs remain hidden by default.\n\n---\n\n# 21. Hermes acceptance\n\nBefore production promotion:\n\n```text\nHermes -> LLM Manager -> qwen3.8-flash-next\n```\n\nmust pass:\n\n1. normal chat;\n2. reasoning response;\n3. tool selection;\n4. proper tool-call schema;\n5. tool result ingestion;\n6. continuation;\n7. multi-turn chat;\n8. 65K+ context;\n9. correct advertised context;\n10. no compression/reset loop.\n\n---\n\n# 22. Production acceptance gate\n\nFlash-Next becomes the primary MIAM-00111 model only if:\n\n```text\ncomplete model retained\nvision smoke passes\n70K context passes\nC4 stable minimum\n>=20 tok/s/agent at useful concurrency\nno host OOM/reclaim storm\nno GPU OOM\nHermes tools pass\nrestart is reproducible\nLLM Manager revision saved\nrollback remains intact\n```\n\nPreferred:\n\n```text\nC6–C8 at >=20 tok/s/agent\n```\n\n---\n\n# 23. Rollback\n\nRollback must be simpler than continuing to debug.\n\nProcedure:\n\n1. stop Flash-Next test VM;\n2. detach/move any shared model disk back if required;\n3. ensure six GPU passthrough devices belong to VM103;\n4. start VM103;\n5. verify Qwen3.8-27B service;\n6. verify `/v1/models`;\n7. run one chat request;\n8. run Hermes tool smoke;\n9. restore normal LLM Manager desired state.\n\nKnown-good endpoint:\n\n```text\nQwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4\nTP2/DP3\n70K\n```\n\n---\n\n# 24. Decision tree\n\n```text\nPreserve VM103\n     |\n     v\nCreate isolated Flash-Next test VM\n     |\n     v\nUse pinned Ampere vLLM + W4A16/FP8-PLE\n     |\n     v\nConfirm ONE shared/offloaded PLE, not six copies\n     |\n     +---- duplicated --> STOP and fix software\n     |\n     v\nTry TP2×PP3/DP1 if patchset cleanly supports PP3\n     |\n     +---- unsupported --> try TP1×PP6 if cleanly supported\n     |                    or TP2×PP2 published-control test\n     |\n     v\n8K C1\n     |\n     v\nVision smoke\n     |\n     v\n32K C1\n     |\n     v\n70K C1\n     |\n     v\nC2/C4/C6/C8\n     |\n     v\n>=20 tok/s/agent + stable Hermes?\n     |\n     +---- YES --> promote\n     |\n     +---- NO --> retain as experimental or rollback\n```\n\n---\n\n# 25. V2 recommendation\n\nThe best path with the hardware already owned is:\n\n```text\n128 GB physical RAM\n~100–108 GB Flash-Next guest\n6× RTX 3080 20 GB = 120 GB nominal VRAM\nfull multimodal Qwen3.8-Flash-Next\nAmpere W4A16 + FP8 PLE checkpoint\npinned vLLM Docker runtime\nPLE CPU offload\nDP1 initially\nall six GPUs through supported TP/PP topology\nMTP off initially\n70K target\n```\n\nThe biggest remaining technical uncertainty is **not the a

Executive recommendation\n\nDo **not** purchase more RAM yet.\n\nThe failed attempt used approximately:\n\n```text\n17.5 GB PLE allocation × 6 workers ≈ 105 GB\n```\n\ninside a roughly 108 GB guest, which caused the OOM. That result shows that the **PLE/n-gram table was being replicated across workers**. It does not show that Qwen3.8-Flash-Next intrinsically requires more than the 128 GB physical RAM installed in MIAM-00111.\n\nA current Ampere-specific Qwen3.8-Flash-Next recipe reports approximately:\n\n```text\nFP8 PLE table:            ~48 GiB\nMeasured total host RAM:  ~85 GiB\nRecommended system RAM:   >=96 GiB\nReference host:           4× RTX 3090\n```\n\nThat makes the present machine a reasonable candidate **if** the PLE is offloaded/shared correctly.\n\nThe constraint is that Proxmox and any other workloads on MIAM-00111 also need memory. Therefore the test VM should be treated as having approximately:\n\n```text\n100–108 GB practical maximum guest RAM\n```\n\nnot the full 128 GB.\n\nThe primary strategy is:\n\n> **Use a separate Flash-Next test VM, preserve the production VM103 unchanged, use a pinned Ampere vLLM container, use an FP8 host-resident PLE, avoid data-parallel PLE duplication, use all six GPUs through pipeline/tensor parallelism, retain the full multimodal model, and increase context/concurrency only after a clean C1 load.**\n\n---\n\n# 1. Correct hardware envelope\n\nCurrent MIAM-00111 inventory should be revalidated at execution, but the expected configuration is:\n\n```text\nSystem RAM:\n8 × 16 GB = 128 GB physical RAM\n\nGPU:\n6 × RTX 3080 20 GB\n= 120 GB nominal aggregate VRAM\n```\n\nImportant:\n\n> Six 20 GB GPUs provide **120 GB nominal VRAM**, not 128 GB.\n\nThe distinction matters because per-GPU memory also matters; 120 GB aggregate does not behave like one unified 120 GB GPU.\n\nBefore starting:\n\n```bash\nfree -h\ndmidecode --type memory\nlscpu\nnumactl -H\nnvidia-smi\nnvidia-smi topo -m\nqm list\n```\n\nRecord:\n\n```text\nphysical RAM available to Proxmox\nall other running VMs/CTs on MIAM-00111\nhost MemAvailable\nguest RAM assigned to VM103\nGPU count and VRAM\nGPU PCIe link width/speed\nNUMA topology\n```\n\n---\n\n# 2. Preserve the current production VM\n\nDo not modify the current known-good VM103 runtime in place.\n\nCurrent rollback target:\n\n```text\nVM103\nQwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4\nvLLM custom fork\nTP2 / DP3\ncontext = 70000\n```\n\nBefore testing Flash-Next:\n\n1. save the exact VM103 Proxmox configuration;\n2. save the exact `vllm.service`;\n3. save `ExecStart`;\n4. save runtime/package/version metadata;\n5. save LLM Manager route and deployment revision;\n6. verify the existing rollback preset still loads as pending configuration;\n7. capture a fresh short health/benchmark result.\n\nVM103 remains the emergency rollback VM.\n\n---\n\n# 3. Preferred isolation: create a separate Flash-Next test VM\n\nA separate VM is preferred because the Flash-Next stack requires unusual pinned vLLM patches, container settings, host-RAM PLE offload, and possibly different NVIDIA/CUDA requirements.\n\nCreate a new VM by cloning the **OS/runtime baseline** of VM103.\n\nDo not change:\n\n```text\nVM103 VMID\nVM103 guest hostname\nVM103 IP\nVM103 DNS/certificates\nVM103 production runtime\n```\n\nThe new test VM receives:\n\n```text\nnew VMID\nnew display name\nnew MAC\ntemporary IP\nonboot = 0 initially\n```\n\nSuggested display label:\n\n```text\nMIAM-00111-QWEN38-FLASHNEXT-TEST\n```\n\n## Important passthrough constraint\n\nThe six GPUs can be assigned to only one active VM at a time.\n\nTherefore:\n\n```text\nVM103 OFF\nFlash-Next test VM ON\n```\n\nor:\n\n```text\nFlash-Next test VM OFF\nVM103 ON\n```\n\nNever start both with the same passthrough devices.\n\nThe test VM is a clean reversible switch, not a concurrently running sibling.\n\n---\n\n# 4. Clone only what is necessary\n\nPreferred approach:\n\n1. clone the VM103 system/root disk;\n2. leave the production VM103 disk untouched;\n3. attach or move the existing 250 GB Flash-Next model/cache disk to the test VM while VM103 is off;\n4. give the test VM the six GPU passthrough devices;\n5. give it the Mellanox/other devices only if actually required;\n6. keep the test VM's network identity separate.\n\nDo **not** duplicate hundreds of gigabytes of model data unnecessarily if the existing staged model disk can be attached exclusively to the test VM.\n\nDocument every disk move so rollback is one command sequence.\n\n---\n\n# 5. Guest RAM budget with 128 GB physical RAM\n\nThe host must retain enough RAM for Proxmox and other host services.\n\n## Initial target\n\nPrefer:\n\n```text\nFlash-Next test VM: 100–104 GB RAM\nballooning: off\n```\n\nOnly increase toward:\n\n```text\n108 GB\n```\n\nif host `MemAvailable`, other guests, and QEMU overhead make it safe.\n\nDo not assign all physical RAM to the guest.\n\n## Host target\n\nTry to preserve roughly:\n\n```text\n20–24 GB class headroom\n```\n\nfor:\n\n```text\nProxmox host\nQEMU overhead\nfilesystem/page cache\nnetwork/storage services\nsafety margin\n```\n\nIf other VMs are running on MIAM-00111, inventory them first.\n\nOnly stop or migrate **nonessential** guests.\n\nDo not stop an unidentified workload merely to gain RAM.\n\n---\n\n# 6. Model completeness / multimodal requirement\n\nQwen3.8-Flash-Next is a multimodal model with a vision encoder.\n\nThis plan preserves the **complete model**, including vision capability.\n\nDo not:\n- strip the vision encoder;\n- delete processor files;\n- use a text-only converted checkpoint as the final target merely to save memory.\n\nThe preferred Ampere recipe has already been validated with **vision enabled**.\n\nFor initial diagnostics, it is acceptable to send only text prompts. That is not the same as removing vision.\n\nAfter text serving is stable, run a real image prompt before production promotion.\n\n---\n\n# 7. Do not use the official FP8 checkpoint directly\n\nThe official FP8 checkpoint is too large for this six-3080 configuration as a straightforward stock-vLLM deployment.\n\nInstead use a tested Ampere-oriented quantized checkpoint such as:\n\n```text\ntodiadiyatmo/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-W4A16-Attn8-FP8PLE\n```\n\nCharacteristics:\n\n```text\nW4A16 main model\nAttn8\nFP8 PLE\nfull multimodal/vision support\nAmpere-focused recipe\n```\n\nThe checkpoint is roughly 124 GB on disk.\n\nThe key is that the large PLE table is **host-resident**, not duplicated in VRAM.\n\n---\n\n# 8. Runtime: vLLM, but isolated and pinned\n\nUse vLLM.\n\nDo **not** try to merge this into `/opt/vllm-venv`.\n\nPreferred runtime:\n\n```text\nDocker / NVIDIA Container Toolkit\npinned vLLM nightly\nexact Ampere recipe patchset\nexact image digest\n```\n\nPrimary reference:\n\n```text\nhttps://github.com/todiadiyatmo/rtx-3090-vllm-recipe-qwen-flash-next\n```\n\nThe reference recipe pins:\n\n```text\nvLLM commit/nightly identity\nAmpere FP8 KV support\nFP8 PLE lookup\npipeline-parallel fixes\nMTP-under-PP fixes\nQwen4Exp support\n```\n\nRecord:\n\n```text\ngit commit\nDocker image digest\npatch SHA256SUMS\ncheckpoint revision\nchat-template revision\nNVIDIA driver\nCUDA version\n```\n\nDo not use `pip install -U vllm` as a deployment method for this experiment.\n\n---\n\n# 9. Critical PLE gate\n\nBefore benchmarking anything, confirm from logs that the runtime resolves the Engram/PLE table as CPU-offloaded.\n\nExpected behavior is equivalent to:\n\n```text\nPLE / Engram CPU offload enabled\n```\n\nand host memory should resemble **one approximately 48 GiB-class FP8 PLE allocation**, not six 17.5 GB worker copies.\n\nMonitor:\n\n```bash\nfree -h\nps -eo pid,cmd,rss --sort=-rss | head -30\ncat /proc/meminfo\ndmesg -T | grep -Ei 'oom|out of memory|killed process'\n```\n\n## Hard stop condition\n\nIf total PLE-related RSS again grows toward:\n\n```text\n~100+ GB\n```\n\nbefore normal model/runtime overhead:\n\n```text\nSTOP\n```\n\nDo not solve a duplicated-PLE software problem by squeezing the host harder.\n\n---\n\n# 10. Use all six GPUs — topology test strategy\n\nThe old failure used:\n\n```text\nTP2 / DP3 / EP\n```\n\nThat should **not** be the first retry because DP created multiple worker/process copies and exposed the PLE duplication problem.\n\nThe Ampere reference is validated on:\n\n```text\nTP2 × PP2\n```\n\nacross four 3090s.\n\nFor six 3080s, test topology conservatively.\n\n## Candidate A — TP2 × PP3 + EP\n\nPreferred first hypothesis:\n\n```text\nTP = 2\nPP = 3\nDP = 1\nEP = enabled\nMTP = off\nall 6 GPUs\n```\n\nWhy:\n\n- model has 2 KV heads, making TP2 natural;\n- PP distributes layers over all six GPUs;\n- DP1 avoids three model/PLE copies;\n- 20 GB cards have less per-GPU VRAM than the reference 24 GB 3090s, so PP3 may help per-stage fit.\n\nThis is **not yet a validated published topology**.\n\nBefore a long startup, verify that the pinned patchset accepts `pipeline_parallel_size=3`.\n\nIf PP3 requires extensive new source changes, stop and move to Candidate B rather than creating a large custom fork.\n\n## Candidate B — TP1 × PP6 + EP\n\nIf supported cleanly:\n\n```text\nTP = 1\nPP = 6\nDP = 1\nEP = enabled\n```\n\nThis distributes layers even more finely.\n\nTrade-offs:\n\n```text\nmore pipeline bubbles\nless tensor communication\npotentially easier VRAM fit\nmore pipeline-stage complexity\n```\n\nTest only if the selected vLLM patchset supports the stage count without invasive changes.\n\n## Candidate C — TP2 × PP2 on four GPUs\n\nUse the published topology as a **recipe-fidelity control**.\n\n```text\nTP = 2\nPP = 2\n4 GPUs active\n2 GPUs idle\n```\n\nBecause RTX 3080 cards have 20 GB rather than 24 GB, this may fail from per-GPU VRAM pressure.\n\nIts purpose is diagnostic:\n\n> Does the published Ampere recipe work on this host at all?\n\nIf Candidate C works but six-GPU variants fail, the problem is topology adaptation, not the basic model/runtime.\n\n---\n\n# 11. Do not use data parallelism initially\n\nDo not begin with:\n\n```text\nDP3\n```\n\nor any topology that creates multiple complete model/PLE worker groups.\n\nFirst get **one model instance** stable across the six GPUs.\n\nConcurrency should initially come from:\n\n```text\nvLLM continuous batching\nmax_num_seqs\nKV/recurrent-state capacity\n```\n\nnot multiple DP copies.\n\nOnce shared PLE behavior is proven, DP can be revisited in a later experiment if there is a strong reason.\n\n---\n\n# 12. GPU-to-GPU communication\n\nThe Ampere reference recipe found GPU P2P materially improved prefill performance but changed decode less.\n\nBefore serving:\n\n```bash\nnvidia-smi topo -m\n```\n\nIf available, run the CUDA P2P bandwidth/latency sample.\n\nRecord whether GPU P2P is actually working.\n\nIf P2P is not available:\n\n```text\ndo not pretend it is\nconfigure NCCL accordingly\n```\n\nA correct PCIe fallback is better than a broken P2P configuration.\n\nDo not patch the production VM's NVIDIA driver for P2P as part of the first Flash-Next bring-up.\n\nProve the model first.\n\n---\n\n# 13. Bring-up ladder\n\nDo not begin with 70K and high concurrency.\n\n## Stage 1 — 8K C1\n\nStart with:\n\n```text\nmax-model-len = 8192\nmax-num-seqs = 1\nMTP = off\nvision model preserved\n```\n\nRequire:\n\n```text\ncontainer healthy\n/v1/models succeeds\nshort text response coherent\nno OOM\nPLE offload correct\nall intended GPUs active\n```\n\n## Stage 2 — vision smoke\n\nWith the same model instance:\n\n```text\none image\nshort question\nsmall output\n```\n\nVerify:\n\n```text\nprocessor works\nvision encoder works\nno unexpected memory explosion\n```\n\nThe reference recipe supports bounded vision requests.\n\n## Stage 3 — 32K C1\n\nRaise:\n\n```text\nmax-model-len = 32768\n```\n\nRun a real ~30K prompt.\n\n## Stage 4 — 70K C1\n\nRaise:\n\n```text\nmax-model-len = 70000\n```\n\nRun:\n\n```text\n65K–68K actual tokenizer tokens\n64–128 output\n```\n\nOnly after this passes should concurrency tuning begin.\n\n---\n\n# 14. MTP strategy\n\nKeep MTP **off** initially.\n\nThe Ampere reference shows:\n\n```text\nMTP off:\nmuch larger logical KV pool\nlower single-stream decode\n\nMTP n=2:\nhigher single-stream decode\nroughly half the KV pool\n```\n\nThe local goal is many concurrent Hermes agents, so KV capacity is more valuable initially.\n\nAfter stable concurrency is known, one optional comparison may test:\n\n```text\nMTP n=2\n```\n\nDo not make it the initial production default.\n\n---\n\n# 15. Concurrency tuning\n\nWith 70K context working, test:\n\n```text\nC1\nC2\nC4\nC6\nC8\n```\n\nFor each run record:\n\n```text\nTTFT p50/p95\nper-agent output tok/s\naggregate output tok/s\nRAM\nVRAM per GPU\nKV capacity\nrequest failures\nGPU utilization\nCPU utilization\n```\n\nPrimary target:\n\n```text\n>=20 output tok/s per active agent\n```\n\nPreferred outcome:\n\n```text\nC6–C8 stable\n```\n\nDo not expect system RAM alone to determine this.\n\nOnce the model fits, concurrency is increasingly controlled by:\n\n```text\nGPU KV/recurrent-state memory\nGPU compute\nbatch scheduler\npipeline efficiency\n```\n\n---\n\n# 16. What 128 GB RAM can realistically accomplish\n\nIf the corrected runtime behaves similarly to the published FP8-PLE Ampere recipe:\n\n```text\n~85 GiB total process/RAM use\n```\n\nthen a 100–108 GB guest is plausible, though tight.\n\nThis leaves approximately:\n\n```text\n15–20+ GB class guest headroom\n```\n\ndepending on exact topology, vision workload, process count, and page cache.\n\nThe host itself must still retain enough memory for Proxmox.\n\nTherefore:\n\n> **128 GB physical RAM is a reasonable software-first target, but this must be proven by measurement on the six-3080 topology.**\n\nNo RAM purchase is part of V2.\n\n---\n\n# 17. If RAM remains the bottleneck\n\nIf the PLE is confirmed non-duplicated but the VM still OOMs:\n\nfirst determine where the memory went.\n\nCapture:\n\n```text\nper-process RSS\nshared memory\npage cache\nPLE worker RSS\nQEMU overhead\nguest kernel memory\nmodel-loader peak\n```\n\nThen try, in order:\n\n1. remove unnecessary test processes;\n2. stop/migrate nonessential guests on MIAM-00111;\n3. reduce build/compile caches during serving;\n4. use the FP8 PLE checkpoint, not BF16 PLE;\n5. reduce initial context while debugging;\n6. reduce `max_num_seqs`;\n7. consider mmap-backed PLE only if supported by the selected vLLM build and proven stable.\n\nDo not purchase RAM as an automatic response.\n\n---\n\n# 18. Multimodal production target\n\nThe final target must preserve:\n\n```text\ntext\nvision/images\nreasoning\ntool calling\nlong context\n```\n\nSuggested bounded vision setting should follow the selected validated recipe.\n\nThe current 3090 recipe has been validated with vision enabled and a bounded image count.\n\nRecord the final:\n\n```text\nimage limit\nmax_pixels\nprocessor revision\n```\n\nin LLM Manager history.\n\n---\n\n# 19. LLM Manager deployment identity\n\nCreate a distinct model/deployment:\n\n```text\nqwen3.8-flash-next\n```\n\nDo not overwrite:\n\n```text\nqwen3.8-27b\ncode\n```\n\nuntil Flash-Next passes final acceptance.\n\nDuring testing, route only explicit test requests to Flash-Next.\n\nAfter promotion, alias changes are a separate controlled action.\n\n---\n\n# 20. LLM Manager history fields for Flash-Next\n\nStore with every successful or failed attempt:\n\n```text\nVMID\nguest RAM\nphysical host RAM\nGPU count/type/VRAM\nartifact/revision/checksum\nvision enabled\nPLE format\nPLE CPU-offload mode\nPLE RSS\nvLLM commit\nDocker digest\npatchset checksum\nTP\nPP\nDP\nEP\nMTP\ncontext\nmax_num_seqs\nKV dtype\nGPU-memory-utilization\nP2P state\nTTFT\nper-agent tok/s\naggregate tok/s\nRAM peak\nVRAM peak per GPU\nHermes tool result\nvision smoke result\nstatus\nfailure reason\n```\n\nFailed runs remain hidden by default.\n\n---\n\n# 21. Hermes acceptance\n\nBefore production promotion:\n\n```text\nHermes -> LLM Manager -> qwen3.8-flash-next\n```\n\nmust pass:\n\n1. normal chat;\n2. reasoning response;\n3. tool selection;\n4. proper tool-call schema;\n5. tool result ingestion;\n6. continuation;\n7. multi-turn chat;\n8. 65K+ context;\n9. correct advertised context;\n10. no compression/reset loop.\n\n---\n\n# 22. Production acceptance gate\n\nFlash-Next becomes the primary MIAM-00111 model only if:\n\n```text\ncomplete model retained\nvision smoke passes\n70K context passes\nC4 stable minimum\n>=20 tok/s/agent at useful concurrency\nno host OOM/reclaim storm\nno GPU OOM\nHermes tools pass\nrestart is reproducible\nLLM Manager revision saved\nrollback remains intact\n```\n\nPreferred:\n\n```text\nC6–C8 at >=20 tok/s/agent\n```\n\n---\n\n# 23. Rollback\n\nRollback must be simpler than continuing to debug.\n\nProcedure:\n\n1. stop Flash-Next test VM;\n2. detach/move any shared model disk back if required;\n3. ensure six GPU passthrough devices belong to VM103;\n4. start VM103;\n5. verify Qwen3.8-27B service;\n6. verify `/v1/models`;\n7. run one chat request;\n8. run Hermes tool smoke;\n9. restore normal LLM Manager desired state.\n\nKnown-good endpoint:\n\n```text\nQwen3.8-27B-AWQ-INT4\nTP2/DP3\n70K\n```\n\n---\n\n# 24. Decision tree\n\n```text\nPreserve VM103\n     |\n     v\nCreate isolated Flash-Next test VM\n     |\n     v\nUse pinned Ampere vLLM + W4A16/FP8-PLE\n     |\n     v\nConfirm ONE shared/offloaded PLE, not six copies\n     |\n     +---- duplicated --> STOP and fix software\n     |\n     v\nTry TP2×PP3/DP1 if patchset cleanly supports PP3\n     |\n     +---- unsupported --> try TP1×PP6 if cleanly supported\n     |                    or TP2×PP2 published-control test\n     |\n     v\n8K C1\n     |\n     v\nVision smoke\n     |\n     v\n32K C1\n     |\n     v\n70K C1\n     |\n     v\nC2/C4/C6/C8\n     |\n     v\n>=20 tok/s/agent + stable Hermes?\n     |\n     +---- YES --> promote\n     |\n     +---- NO --> retain as experimental or rollback\n```\n\n---\n\n# 25. V2 recommendation\n\nThe best path with the hardware already owned is:\n\n```text\n128 GB physical RAM\n~100–108 GB Flash-Next guest\n6× RTX 3080 20 GB = 120 GB nominal VRAM\nfull multimodal Qwen3.8-Flash-Next\nAmpere W4A16 + FP8 PLE checkpoint\npinned vLLM Docker runtime\nPLE CPU offload\nDP1 initially\nall six GPUs through supported TP/PP topology\nMTP off initially\n70K target\n

{"status": "success", "output": "rc: 0\n# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0\n# SPDX-FileCopyrightText: Copyright contributors to the vLLM project\n\"\"\"Model-runner state for Qwen4Exp PLE inputs.\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\n\nfrom vllm.config import VllmConfig\nfrom vllm.v1.worker.gpu.input_batch import InputBatch\nfrom vllm.v1.worker.gpu.mm.encoder_cache import EncoderCache\nfrom vllm.v1.worker.gpu.model_states.mamba_hybrid import MambaHybridModelState\nfrom vllm.v1.worker.gpu.states import RequestState\n\n\nclass Qwen4ExpModelState(MambaHybridModelState):\n    \"\"\"Add rollback-safe PLE n-gram context to the model inputs.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        vllm_config: VllmConfig,\n        model: nn.Module,\n        encoder_cache: EncoderCache | None,\n        device: torch.device,\n    ) -> None:\n        super().__init__(vllm_config, model, encoder_cache, device)\n        config = self.model_config.hf_text_config\n        self.uses_ngram_embedding = bool(config.ple_layer_ids)\n        if not self.uses_ngram_embedding:\n            self.ngram_context_len = 0\n            self.ngram_eos_token_id = 0\n            return\n\n        if vllm_config.parallel_config.pipeline_parallel_size > 1:\n            raise RuntimeError(\n                \"N-gram PLE embedding currently requires \"\n                \"pipeline_parallel_size=1 because non-first pipeline ranks do \"\n                \"not receive the raw input_ids required by PLE. Please run \"\n                \"with PP=1.\"\n            )\n\n        self.ngram_context_len = int(config.ngram_size) - 1\n        if self.ngram_context_len <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"N-gram embedding requires context length >= 1.\")\n        self.ngram_eos_token_id = int(config.eos_token_id)\n        # PLE runs inside captured regions, so these buffers keep a fixed shape\n        # and address as the active request count changes between replays.\n        self.ngram_context = torch.full(\n            (self.max_num_reqs, self.ngram_context_len),\n            self.ngram_eos_token_id,\n            dtype=torch.int32,\n            device=self.device,\n        )\n        self.ngram_context_offsets = torch.arange(\n            -self.ngram_context_len,\n            0,\n            dtype=torch.int64,\n            device=self.device,\n        )\n        self.ple_query_start_loc = torch.zeros(\n            self.max_num_reqs + 1,\n            dtype=torch.int32,\n            device=self.device,\n        )\n\n    def _prepare_ngram_context(\n        self,\n        input_batch: InputBatch,\n        req_states: RequestState,\n    ) -> torch.Tensor:\n        num_reqs = input_batch.num_reqs\n        context = self.ngram_context\n        context.fill_(self.ngram_eos_token_id)\n        if num_reqs == 0:\n            return context\n\n        request_indices = input_batch.idx_mapping[:num_reqs].long()\n        context_end = req_states.num_computed_tokens.gpu[request_indices].long()\n        token_indices = context_end.unsqueeze(1) + self.ngram_context_offsets\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 2.41}

The same rig also runs a llama.cpp GGUF\nfallback lane; both lanes are documented with the same yardstick.\n\n## Results (2026-09-02, same rig, same context window, same model)\n\n| | llama.cpp GGUF lane | **vLLM PP3 lane (prod)** |\n|---|---|---|\n| Weights | UD-Q4_K_XL, ~104 GB across 2× 170HX | AWQ-INT4, ~176 GB across 3090 + 2× 170HX |\n| Context | 262,144 | 262,144 (native; to 737K on the YaRN lane |\n| Concurrency | 1 sequence | 4 sequences — warm aggregate: 96 (2 streams) no-MTP / 89 with MTP |\n| Tool calling | — | OpenAI tools + `tool_choice` (parser: `qwen3_xml`, matches the model's XML template) |\n| Vision | via `mmproj-F16.gguf` sidecar | native, in-checkpoint ViT |\n| Cold boot | ~3 min | ~11 min |\n| Crash recovery | manual | auto (`--restart unless-stopped`) |\n\nRaw evidence of the passing 256K verification: [`results/verify-256k.log`](results/verify-256k.log).\n\n## Repo layout\n\n```\nscripts/\n  serve-vllm-pp3-262k-mtp-rank-reorder.sh # PROD since 2026-09-10: 262K + MTP-3, PP0 on the 64G sm_80 card\n  serve-vllm-pp3-262k-mtp.sh # the prior prod lane: 262K native + MTP-3 (sanitized)\n  serve-vllm-pp3-786k.sh    # the 786K YaRN 3.0 no-MTP lane (rollback / long-context day)\n  serve-vllm-pp3-256k.sh    # the original no-MTP recipe the above derive from\n  site26-pp-draft-table-sync.py  # the PP draft-table ring-sync patch (MTP at PP>1)\n  serve-llamacpp-gguf.sh    # the llama.cpp GGUF fallback lane\n  verify-256k.sh            # boot diag -> quality -> REAL 250K-token request -> vision\n  bench-mtp-pp3.sh          # speculative-decoding bench launcher\nresults/\n  verify-256k.log           # passing run output\ndocs/\n  deployment/               # full deployment log: bring-up, PP4->PP3 partitioning,\n                            # deadlock hunt, lane-swap playbook, isolation probes\n  pp-debug/                 # MTP speculative-decoding forensics: NCCL deadlock\n                            # patches (sites 15/17/18), corruption hunt, fork bug map\n```\n\n## Quickstart (vLLM lane)\n\nPrereqs: Docker + NVIDIA container toolkit; the fork image (see caveat below); the\nAWQ checkpoint at a path you mount as `/model`.\n\n```bash\nexport VLLM_API_KEY=your-secret      # scripts default to 'change-me'\nbash scripts/serve-vllm-pp3-262k-mtp.sh  # ~9-11 min to come up\nbash scripts/verify-256k.sh           # full verification incl. a real 250K-token request\n```\n\n**Image caveat:** the recipe uses image `qwen38-flash-next:pp3fix22` — a locally built\nvLLM fork image (CUDA 13 production base) carrying FlashNext/Qwen4Exp architecture\nsupport, PLE CPU offload, Triton GDN decode kernels (baked in as image env), our PP3\ndeadlock fixes (sites 8–10, 13, 17–18, 23), and the site-26 draft-table ring sync\nthat makes MTP work under pipeline parallelism.\nIt is not on a registry; the patches it contains are in\n[`docs/pp-debug/references/site15-17-18-patches.md`](docs/pp-debug/references/site15-17-18-patches.md),\n[`docs/pp-debug/references/spec-corruption-hunt.md`](docs/pp-debug/references/spec-corruption-hunt.md),\nand [`scripts/site26-pp-draft-table-sync.py`](scripts/site26-pp-draft-table-sync.py)\nso you can recreate or port them.\n\n## The 256K context math (why it fits on 24 GB + 104 GB)\n\nFull derivation in [`docs/pp-debug/references/context-and-vision-math.md`](docs/pp-debug/references/context-and-vision-math.md). Short version:\n\n- The architecture is hybrid: only **12 of 48 layers** are full attention (1-in-4\n  interval). The other 36 are GDN — constant-size state per sequence, no per-token KV.\n- Full-attn layers have 2 KV heads × 256 dim, bf16. At partition `8,28,12`:\n  per-token KV is **PP0 = 4 KB, PP1 = 14 KB, PP2 = 6 KB**.\n- Default pools at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.85` hold ~603K / 588K / 2M tokens;\n  the binding rank (PP1, the 64 GB card) holds 588K tokens against a 262,144 need —\n  **2.2× headroom**, no partition surgery required.\n- With MTP-3 on, the drafter (weights + draft KV, last rank) costs ~38K tokens of\n  pool (~9%): measured 488,863-token pool = **1.86× the window** at util 0.85.\n- **Do not pass `--kv-cache-memory`** with asymmetric partitions: it caps every rank\n  globally and strangles the fat rank below the target.\n- First multi-sequence generations after boot measure ~4x slow (CUDA-graph capture per batch shape happens on first use) — benchmark warm.\n- 256K boots transiently OOM-retry on the 24 GB rank during memory profiling\n  (expandable-segments warnings) — benign, it recovers. Boot takes ~11 min.\n- If you OOM for real, the ladder is: `--max-num-batched-tokens 4096` →\n  repartition `6,30,12` → drop to 192K.\n- **Prefix caching is disabled deliberately.** On this GDN architecture, long\n  multi-turn **vision** conversations (agent loop: screenshot each step, growing\n  history) reproducibly wedge the engine core at ~step 8-9 — `/v1/models` stays\n  200 while completions hang forever (3/3 reproduced; 12/12 steps pass with the\n  flag off). Log signature: Triton JIT of `_bilinear_pos_embed_kernel` mid-flight.\n  Matches vLLM issue #45238 (GDN prefix-cache). Cost of leaving it off: late-step\n  re-prefill (~+1-3 s/step at ~30K ctx). Re-enable only after the upstream fix.\n\n## MTP speculative decoding: fixed and in production (site-26)\n\nMTP k=3 has been **on in production since 2026-09-02**: 85 tok/s warm single-stream\n(57–59 no-MTP), 55.6% draft acceptance, clean text, clean stop behavior.\n\nThe last blocker after the 37-build campaign: under pipeline parallelism the\nspeculator's `propose()` runs only on the last rank, so the other ranks verified\nagainst zeroed draft-token tables — corrupted text, 0% acceptance. **Site-26** adds a\nthird broadcast to the site-17/18 input ring that distributes the draft-token table\nto every rank before verification:\n[`scripts/site26-pp-draft-table-sync.py`](scripts/site26-pp-draft-table-sync.py).\nNo public recipe existed for MTP-at-PP; this is the missing piece.\n\nValidation numbers, gotchas (thinking-budget needle artifact, concurrency spec\ndiscount), rollback, and the tested-and-parked 786K+MTP verdict:\n[`docs/deployment/262k-mtp-prod.md`](docs/deployment/262k-mtp-prod.md).\nThe full debugging campaign history is in [`docs/pp-debug/`](docs/pp-debug/).\n\n########## todiadiyatmo/rtx-3090-vllm-recipe-qwen-flash-next ##########\n404: Not Found\n########## loktar00/qwen38-flash-next-vllm-3090-recipe ##########\n# Qwen3.8-Flash-Next W4A16 on 4x RTX 3090\n\nServing the 180B Qwen3.8-Flash-Next W4A16 checkpoint on four 24 GB Ampere cards with vLLM built from\na pinned PR head plus five patches, every card capped at 220 W. 116 t/s single stream on the essay\nbench, up to 198 t/s peak on short code replies, 337,677 KV tokens at 262k context, and it beats the\n4x3090 reference recipe on every row but one.\n\n## Profiles\n\n| profile | ctx | KV tokens | decode t/s | c8 aggregate | for |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `mtp` | 65,536 | 76,046 | 116.6 @4k / 115.1 @32k | 158.3 | fast single stream |\n| `fp8mtp128k` | 131,072 | 142,096 | 107.9 @4k / 111.7 @32k / 128.8 @120k (essay medians); 186 avg, 198 peak on code | 198.0 | MTP speed past 65k, up to 128k |\n| `bf16` | 131,072 | 178,465 | 72.6 | 180.7-230.3 | batch work and prompts over 64k |\n| `fp8deep` | 262,144 | 337,677 | 68.1 @260k | not measured | 130k-260k prompts |\n\nReceipts in row order: `results/fp8/bf16_ref`, `results/128k/`, `results/iter/r10_nospec_full` +\n`results/v4`, `results/fp8/fp8_262k`, plus `results/peak/` and `results/128k/essay/`. Both FP8\nprofiles need the optional `fp8kv/` patch and a calibrated `scales.json`. That loader is fail-closed\nand refuses to start without one.\n\n## vs alesha-pro/qwen38-flash-next-4x3090\n\nSame four cards, same 220 W cap, their published numbers on the right.\n\n| | here | reference | |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| decode @4k | 116.6 (`mtp`) | 65.8 | win |\n| decode @32k | 115.1 (`mtp`) | 65.5 | win |\n| decode @128k | 72.75 (131,068 tok) | 69.2 (130,945) | win |\n| decode @260k | 68.11 (260,507 tok) | 66.3 (260,578) | win |\n| TTFT @260k | 201-206 s | 162.9 s | **loss** |\n| prefill @32k | 3,187 | 1,955 | win |\n| prefill @128k | 2,835 | 1,836 | win |\n| c8 aggregate | 180.7-230.3 (`bf16`) | 88.2 | win |\n| KV @131k BF16 | 178,465 | 139,628 | win |\n| KV @262k FP8 | 337,677 | 256,840 | win |\n\nBoth 128k rows are the FP8 KV lane. On BF16 KV that prefill reads 1,548 t/s and loses. The reference\nserves `--max-num-seqs 1`, which is most of the c8 gap.\n\n## Peak\n\nShort prompts, 128k profile, single stream, reasoning_effort medium, meter reading vLLM's own\ntoken counters every 5 s (`results/peak/`). The reference recipe publishes no peak, so this is not in\nthe comparison table.\n\n| prompt | avg t/s over the reply | best 5-s window |\n|---|---|---|\n| code, 3,000 tok | 186.4 / 187.6 | 198.5 / 193.5 |\n| list, 1,500 tok | 118.2 / 120.7 | 129.4 / 127.0 |\n| prose, 900 tok | 109.3 / 102.1 | 117.8 / 106.3 |\n\nTTFT 0.29-0.32 s. Code vs prose is MTP acceptance. Depth costs little: the essay bench on this\nprofile reads 107.9 @4k, 111.7 @32k, 128.8 @120k.\n\n## Quick start\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/loktar00/qwen38-flash-next-vllm-3090-recipe\ncd qwen38-flash-next-vllm-3090-recipe\ncp .env.example .env && $EDITOR .env        # MODEL and GPUS at minimum\nset -a; . ./.env; set +a                    # install.sh reads env, it does not source\n./install.sh                                # venv + vLLM from source, ~40 min\n\nHF_XET_HIGH_PERFORMANCE=1 hf download VnimanieAI/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-W4A16 \\\n  --revision 9236d703b25f25eb5c17e9640204f84fa1ce0c6e\n\nPROFILE=mtp ./serve.sh                      # bf16 | mtp | fp8mtp128k | fp8deep\nuntil curl -sf 127.0.0.1:8899/health; do sleep 10; done\npython3 bench/bench.py runs/verify m1       # has to answer 391\n```\n\nThe streaming t/s script is `AGENTS.md` section 6.3, standalone, no dependencies. llama-swap is\noptional; `serve.sh` runs every profile on its own, and `llama-swap/` has the entries if you use it.\nAgents: point yours at `AGENTS.md` (`CLAUDE.md` for Claude Code).\n\n## Why it is fast\n\nvLLM instead of llama.cpp, where the same weights as a UD-Q4_K_XL GGUF run 56-58 t/s, host dispatch\nbound. The checkpoint carries its own MTP draft head and k=4 is the acceptance peak.\n`cudagraph_mode=FULL` rather than the `FULL_AND_PIECEWISE` default, which leaves the drafter eager.\n\nSpread is +-15% run to run on decode.
